2.1.3
Voltage Equation of the Synchronous Generator in Per Unit System

A normalization of variables called the per unit normalization is
always desirable. The idea is to pick base values for quantities such as voltages,
currents, impedances, power, and so on, and to define the quantity in per unit as

\mathrm{quantity\ in\ per\ unit}=\frac{\mathrm{actual\ quantity}}{\mathrm{base\ value\ of\ quantity}}

(2.10)

By carefully choosing the base quantities for both stator and rotor variables, the
electrical dynamics expressed by
Equation 2.9
can be expressed
in the p.u. system as

\begin{bmatrix}L_{d}&kM_{F}&kM_{D}&0&0\\
-kM_{F}&-L_{F}&-M_{R}&0&0\\
-kM_{D}&-M_{R}&-L_{D}&0&0\\
0&0&0&L_{q}&kM_{Q}\\
0&0&0&-kM_{Q}&-L_{Q}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{i_{d}}\\
\dot{i_{F}}\\
\dot{i_{D}}\\
\dot{i_{q}}\\
\dot{i_{Q}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}-r&0&0&-\omega L_{q}&-\omega kM_{Q}\\
0&r_{F}&0&0&0\\
0&0&r_{D}&0&0\\
\omega L_{d}&\omega kM_{F}&\omega kM_{D}&-r&0\\
0&0&0&0&r_{Q}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}i_{d}\\
i_{F}\\
i_{D}\\
i_{q}\\
i_{Q}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}v_{d}\\
v_{F}\\
0\\
v_{q}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\ \mathrm{p.u.}

(2.11)

It is obvious that
Equation 2.9
and
Equation 2.11
are identical. This is
always possible if base quantities are carefully chosen. The derivation of

Equation 2.11
can be found in
[
1
]
.

2.2
A Synchronous Generator Connected to an Infinite Bus

A typical configuration of a generation system model is a synchronous generator
connected to an infinite bus as shown in
Figure 2.4
. The figure
shows a synchronous generator connected to an infinite bus through a transmission line having
resistance
R_{e}
and inductance
L_{e}
. Only the voltages and currents for phase
a
are
shown, where
v_{a}
is the phase voltage,
i_{a}
is the phase current, and
V_{\infty}
is the infinite bus voltage. An infinite bus is
an approximation of a large interconnected power system, where the action of a single
generator will not affect the operation of the power grid. In an infinite bus, the system
frequency is constant, independent of power flow, and the system voltage is constant,
independent of reactive power consumed or supplied.

-5,-4)(5,1)

\coil
[dipolestyle=curved](0,0)(2,0)
L_{e}

\resistor
[dipolestyle=zigzag](-2,0)(0,0)
R_{e}

Figure 2.4:
Synchronous generator loaded by an infinite bus.

The constraints of the infinite bus are given by

\begin{bmatrix}v_{d}\\
v_{q}\end{bmatrix}=R_{e}\begin{bmatrix}i_{d}\\
i_{q}\end{bmatrix}+L_{e}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{i_{d}}\\
\dot{i_{q}}\end{bmatrix}-\omega L_{e}\begin{bmatrix}-i_{q}\\
i_{d}\end{bmatrix}+\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}\begin{bmatrix}-\sin{(\delta-\alpha)}\\
\cos{(\delta-\alpha)}\end{bmatrix}

(2.12)

By including
Equation 2.12
, we can rewrite
Equation 2.11
as

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}L_{d}+L_{e}&kM_{F}&kM_{D}&0&0\\
-kM_{F}&-L_{F}&-M_{R}&0&0\\
-kM_{D}&-M_{R}&-L_{D}&0&0\\
0&0&0&L_{q}+L_{e}&kM_{Q}\\
0&0&0&-kM_{Q}&-L_{Q}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{i_{d}}\\
\dot{i_{F}}\\
\dot{i_{D}}\\
\dot{i_{q}}\\
\dot{i_{Q}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}-r-R_{e}&0&0&-\omega(L_{q}+L_{e})&-\omega kM_{Q}\\
0&r_{F}&0&0&0\\
0&0&r_{D}&0&0\\
\omega(L_{d}+L_{e})&\omega kM_{F}&\omega kM_{D}&-r-R_{e}&0\\
0&0&0&0&r_{Q}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}i_{d}\\
i_{F}\\
i_{D}\\
i_{q}\\
i_{Q}\end{bmatrix}

(2.13)

\displaystyle-\begin{bmatrix}0\\
v_{F}\\
0\\
0\\
0\end{bmatrix}-\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}\begin{bmatrix}-\sin{(\delta-\alpha)}\\
0\\
0\\
\cos{(\delta-\alpha)}\\
0\end{bmatrix}

Thus,
Equation 2.13
describes the electrical dynamics of a synchronous generator
connected to an infinite bus.

2.3
Mechanical Dynamics: Swing Equation

In this subsection, we present the mechanical
dynamics of the synchronous generator.
Under normal operating conditions, the relative position of the rotor axis and the
resultant magnetic field axis is fixed. The angle between the two is known as the

power angle
or
torque angle
. During any disturbance, the rotor will
decelerate or accelerate with respect to the synchronously rotating air gap magneto-motive
force (mmf), which is any physical driving (motive) force that produces magnetic flux, and
a relative motion begins. In this context, the expression ’driving force’ is used in a
general sense of work potential, and is analogous, but distinct, from force measured in
Newton’s. In magnetic circuits the magneto-motive
force (mmf) plays a role analogous to the role emf (voltage) plays in
electric circuits.
The equation describing this relative motion is known as the

swing equation

[
1
]
. If, after this oscillatory period, the rotor locks back
into synchronous speed, the generator will maintain its stability. If the disturbance does
not involve any net change in power, the rotor returns to its original position. If the
disturbance is created by a change in generation, load, or in network conditions, the
rotor comes to a new operating power angle relative to the synchronously revolving field.

The swing equation thus governs the motion of the machine rotor relating the moment of
inertia (also referred to as the rotational inertia of the rotor) to the resultant of the
mechanical and electrical torques on the rotor, i.e.,
J\ddot{\theta}=T_{a}\ \mathrm{N\cdot m}
, where
J
is the moment of
inertia of all rotating masses attached to the shaft,
\theta
is the mechanical angle of
the shaft with
respect to a fixed reference, and
T_{a}
is the accelerating torque acting on the shaft. The torque is given by

T_{a}=T_{m}-T_{e}-T_{d}
, where
T_{m}
,
T_{e}
, and
T_{d}
are mechanical,
electrical, and damping torques, respectively. The mechanical torque
T_{m}
is the driving
torque provided by the prime mover. The electrical torque
T_{e}
is generated by the load
currents of the armature windings on the stator. The damping torque
T_{d}
is produced by
the damper windings on the rotor. The angular
reference may be chosen relative to a synchronously rotating reference frame moving with
constant angular velocity
\omega_{mR}
. The rotor angle in the static frame is
given by
\theta(t)=(\omega_{mR}t+\beta)+\delta_{m}
, where

\beta
is a constant and
\delta_{m}
is the rotor position also referred to as the

mechanical torque angle
, measured from the synchronously rotating
reference frame.
Let us denote the
shaft angular velocity
in the static frame as
\omega_{m}
in rad/sec, thus we
have
\omega_{m}=\dot{\theta}=\omega_{mR}+\dot{\delta}_{m}
. By taking the
derivative of
\omega_{m}
and second derivative of
\theta
we obtain

\dot{\omega}_{m}=\ddot{\theta}=\ddot{\delta}_{m}
, if we substitute
this in
J\ddot{\theta}=T_{a}
we have

J\ddot{\theta}=J\ddot{\delta}_{m}=J\dot{\omega}_{m}=T_{a}=T_{m}-T_{e}-T_{d}\ [\mathrm{N\cdot m}]

(2.14)

The product of torque and
angular velocity is the shaft power in watts, thus we have

J\ddot{\delta}_{m}\omega_{m}=P_{m}-P_{e}-P_{d}\ \ [\mathrm{W}]

(2.15)

The quantity
J\omega_{m}
is called the inertia constant and is denoted by
M
. It is
related to the kinetic energy of the rotating masses
W_{k}
, where
W_{k}=\frac{1}{2}J\omega^{2}_{m}
.
M
is computed as

M=J\omega_{m}=\frac{2W_{k}}{\omega_{m}}\ [\mathrm{J\cdot s}]

(2.16)

Although
M
is called an inertia constant, it is not really constant when the rotor speed
deviates from the synchronous speed
\omega_{mR}
. However, since
\omega_{m}
does not
change by a large amount before stability is lost,
M
is evaluated at the synchronous
speed
\omega_{mR}
and is considered to remain constant, i.e.,

M=J\omega_{m}\cong\frac{2W_{k}}{\omega_{mR}}\ [\mathrm{J\cdot s}]

(2.17)

The swing equation in terms of the inertia constant becomes

M\ddot{\delta}_{m}=M\dot{\omega}_{m}=P_{m}-P_{e}-P_{d}

(2.18)

In relating the machine inertial performance to the network, it would be more useful to
write
Equation 2.18
in terms of an electrical angle that can be conveniently related
to the position of the rotor. Such an angle is the torque angle
\delta
, which is the
angle between the magneto-motive force (mmf) and the resultant magneto-motive force (mmf) in the air gap, both rotating at synchronous
speed. It is also the electrical angle between the generated emf and the resultant stator
voltage phasors. The torque angle
\delta
, which is the same as the
electrical angle, is related to the rotor mechanical angle
\delta_{m}
, (measured from a
synchronously rotating frame) by

\delta=\frac{p}{2}\delta_{m}

(2.19)

where
p
is the number of poles of the synchronous generator.
Figure 2.1
shows a
schematic of a synchronous generator with two poles. Also, the synchronous speed
\omega_{mR}
used in the previous equations
is actually the mechanical synchronous speed or the mechanical angular velocity at the synchronous reference value.
It is related to the electrical synchronous speed
\omega_{R}
by

\omega_{R}=\frac{p}{2}\omega_{mR}

(2.20)

By taking the derivative of

Equation 2.19
on both sides, we get

\dot{\delta}=\frac{p}{2}\dot{\delta}_{m}

(2.21)

Adding
Equation 2.20
and
Equation 2.21
we get

\dot{\delta}+\omega_{R}=\frac{p}{2}\dot{\delta}_{m}+\frac{p}{2}\omega_{mR}\\

(2.22)

Thus, the electrical angular velocity

\omega
is related to the mechanical angular velocity
\omega_{m}
by

\omega=\dot{\delta}+\omega_{R}=\frac{p}{2}(\dot{\delta}_{m}+\omega_{mR})=\frac{p}{2}\omega_{m}

(2.23)

Combining

Equation 2.18
and
Equation 2.19
we get

\frac{2M}{p}\ddot{\delta}=\frac{2M}{p}\dot{\omega}=P_{m}-P_{e}-P_{d}

(2.24)

Thus, we can rewrite
Equation 2.24
as follows:

\ddot{\delta}=\dot{\omega}=-\frac{p}{2M}P_{d}+\frac{p}{2M}(P_{m}-P_{e})

(2.25)

Since power system analysis is done in p.u. system, the swing equation is usually
expressed in per unit. Dividing
Equation 2.24
by the base power
S_{B}
, and substituting
for
M
results in

\frac{2}{p}\frac{2W_{k}}{\omega_{mR}S_{B}}\ddot{\delta}=\frac{2}{p}\frac{2W_{k}}{\omega_{mR}S_{B}}\dot{\omega}=\frac{P_{m}}{S_{B}}-\frac{P_{e}}{S_{B}}-\frac{P_{d}}{S_{B}}

(2.26)

We now define an important quantity known as the p.u. inertia constant
H
[
8
]
.

H=\frac{W_{k}}{S_{B}}

(2.27)

The unit of
H
is seconds. The value of
H
ranges from 1 to 10 seconds, depending
on the size and type of machine. The per unit accelerating power is related to the per unit
accelerating torque by
P_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}=T_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}\frac{\omega}{\omega_{R}}
. Recognizing that the electrical angular speed
\omega

is nearly constant, and equal to
\omega_{R}
, we have the p.u. accelerating
power
P_{a}
to be numerically nearly equal to the p.u. accelerating torque
T_{a}
, i.e.

P_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}\cong T_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}
.
Substituting
for
H
, and
P_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}\cong T_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}
in
Equation 2.26
, we get

\frac{2}{p}\frac{2H}{\omega_{mR}}\ddot{\delta}=\frac{2}{p}\frac{2H}{\omega_{mR}}\dot{\omega}=P_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}=P_{m\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-P_{e\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-P_{d\mathrm{(p.u.)}}\cong T_{a\mathrm{(p.u.)}}=T_{m\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-T_{e\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-T_{d\mathrm{(p.u.)}}

(2.28)

where
P_{m\mathrm{(p.u.)}}
,
P_{e\mathrm{(p.u.)}}
, and
P_{d\mathrm{(p.u.)}}
are the
per unit mechanical power, electrical power, and damping power respectively.
Substituting
\omega_{R}=\frac{p}{2}\omega_{mR}
in
Equation 2.28
we get

\frac{2H}{\omega_{R}}\ddot{\delta}=\frac{2H}{\omega_{R}}\dot{\omega}=P_{m\mathrm{(p.u.})}-P_{e\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-P_{d\mathrm{(p.u.)}}\cong T_{m\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-T_{e\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-T_{d\mathrm{(p.u.)}}

(2.29)

In
Equation 2.29
, while the torque is normalized, the angular speed
\omega
and the time
t
are not in per unit.
Thus the equation is not completely in per unit. We know that the angular speed
\omega
and time
t
in per unit are given by

\displaystyle\omega_{(p.u.)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega}{\omega_{R}}

(2.30)

\displaystyle t_{(p.u.)}

\displaystyle=\omega_{R}t

where the base angular velocity
\omega_{B}=\omega_{R}
.
Substituting
Equation 2.30
in
Equation 2.29
the normalized swing equation can be written as

\tau_{j}\frac{d\omega_{(p.u.)}}{dt_{(p.u.)}}=T_{m\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-T_{e\mathrm{(p.u.)}}-T_{d\mathrm{(p.u.)}}

(2.31)

where
\tau_{j}=2H\omega_{R}
.
The damping torque is calculated as
T_{d\mathrm{(p.u.)}}=D\omega
, where

D
is the damping constant. The electrical torque
T_{e\phi}
is calculated as

T_{e\phi}=i_{q}\lambda_{d}-i_{d}\lambda_{q}

(2.32)

Also
T_{e}=\frac{T_{e\phi}}{3}
, where
T_{e}
is the per unit electromagnetic torque defined on a three phase
(3\phi)
VA base, and

T_{e\phi}
is the per unit electromagnetic torque defined on a per phase VA base.
Substituting
Equation 2.6
and
Equation 2.7
into

Equation 2.32
and writing
T_{e}
in the p.u. system, we obtain

T_{e\phi\mathrm{(p.u.)}}=3T_{e(p.u.)}=L_{d}i_{d}i_{q}+kM_{F}i_{F}i_{q}+kM_{D}i_{D}i_{q}-L_{q}i_{d}i_{q}-kM_{Q}i_{d}i_{Q}

(2.33)

From
Equation 2.23
we have
\omega=\dot{\delta}+\omega_{R}
. If we choose
\omega_{R}
as the frequency base and divide
both sides of this equation by
\omega_{R}
we have

\frac{\omega}{\omega_{R}}=\frac{\dot{\delta}}{\omega_{R}}+\frac{\omega_{R}}{\omega_{R}}

(2.34)

Since,
\omega_{\mathrm{(p.u.)}}=\frac{\omega}{\omega_{R}}
and
\dot{\delta}_{\mathrm{(p.u.)}}=\frac{\dot{\delta}}{\omega_{R}}

we can write
Equation 2.34
as

\dot{\delta}_{(pu)}=\omega_{(pu)}-1
. Thus, from
Equation 2.29
,
Equation 2.33
, and
Equation 2.34

we can write the mechanical dynamics in the p.u. system as

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}\frac{(L_{d}i_{d}i_{q}+kM_{F}i_{F}i_{q}+kM_{D}i_{D}i_{q}-L_{q}i_{d}i_{q}-kM_{Q}i_{d}i_{Q})}{3}-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}D\omega+\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}T_{m}

(2.35)

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}

\displaystyle=\omega-1

2.4
Truth Model of the Synchronous Generator

By combining the electrical dynamics and mechanical dynamics, we obtain the
truth model of the synchronous generator which is highly nonlinear. Let us define

L=\begin{bmatrix}L_{d}+L_{e}&kM_{F}&kM_{D}&0&0\\
-kM_{F}&-L_{F}&-M_{R}&0&0\\
-kM_{D}&-M_{R}&-L_{D}&0&0\\
0&0&0&L_{q}+L_{e}&kM_{Q}\\
0&0&0&-kM_{Q}&-L_{Q}\end{bmatrix}

Also denote
\mu=(L_{d}+L_{e})M_{R}^{2}-L_{D}L_{F}(L_{d}+L_{e})+k^{2}(L_{D}M_{F}^{2}+L_{F}M_{D}^{2}-2M_{D}M_{F}M_{R})
and
\nu=-k^{2}M_{Q}^{2}+L_{Q}(L_{e}+L_{q})
, we can derive the inverse matrix of

L
as

\displaystyle L^{-1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{R}^{2}-L_{D}L_{F})&\frac{k}{\mu}(M_{D}M_{R}-L_{D}M_{F})&\frac{k}{\mu}(M_{F}M_{R}-L_{F}M_{D})&0&0\\
-\frac{k}{\mu}(M_{D}M_{R}-L_{D}M_{F})&-\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{D}^{2}k^{2}-L_{D}(L_{d}+L_{e}))&-\frac{1}{\mu}((L_{d}+L_{e})M_{R}-M_{D}M_{F}k^{2})&0&0\\
-\frac{k}{\mu}(M_{F}M_{R}-L_{F}M_{D})&-\frac{1}{\mu}((L_{d}+L_{e})M_{R}-M_{D}M_{F}k^{2})&-\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{F}^{2}k^{2}-L_{F}(L_{d}+L_{e}))&0&0\\
0&0&0&\frac{L_{Q}}{\nu}&\frac{kM_{Q}}{\nu}\\
0&0&0&-\frac{kM_{Q}}{\nu}&-\frac{L_{e}+L_{q}}{\nu}\end{bmatrix}

(2.36)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}L_{d1}&kM_{F1}&kM_{D1}&0&0\\
-kM_{F1}&-L_{F1}&-M_{R1}&0&0\\
-kM_{D1}&-M_{R1}&-L_{D1}&0&0\\
0&0&0&L_{q1}&kM_{Q1}\\
0&0&0&-kM_{Q1}&-L_{Q1}\end{bmatrix}

where
L_{d1}=\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{R}^{2}-L_{D}L_{F})
,
L_{F1}=\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{D}^{2}k^{2}-L_{D}(L_{d}+L_{e}))
,
L_{D1}=\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{F}^{2}k^{2}-L_{F}(L_{d}+L_{e}))
,
M_{F1}=\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{D}M_{R}-L_{D}M_{F})
,
M_{D1}=\frac{1}{\mu}(M_{F}M_{R}-L_{F}M_{D})
,
M_{R1}=\frac{1}{\mu}((L_{d}+L_{e})M_{R}-M_{D}M_{F}k^{2})
,
L_{q1}=\frac{L_{Q}}{\nu}
,
L_{Q1}=\frac{L_{e}+L_{q}}{\nu}
, and

M_{Q1}=\frac{M_{Q}}{\nu}
.
Using
Equation 2.36
and

Equation 2.13
we can write

\displaystyle\dot{i}_{d}=-L_{d1}(r+R_{e})i_{d}+kM_{F1}r_{F}i_{F}+kM_{D1}r_{D}i_{D}-(L_{q}+L_{e})L_{d1}i_{q}\omega-kM_{Q}L_{d1}i_{Q}\omega

(2.37)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}L_{d1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)-kM_{F1}v_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{i}_{F}=kM_{F1}(r+R_{e})i_{d}-L_{F1}r_{F}i_{F}-M_{R1}r_{D}i_{D}+kM_{F1}(L_{q}+L_{e})i_{q}\omega+k^{2}M_{F1}M_{Q}i_{Q}\omega

\displaystyle-\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}kM_{F1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+L_{F1}v_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{i}_{D}=kM_{D1}(r+R_{e})i_{d}-M_{R1}r_{F}i_{F}-L_{D1}r_{D}i_{D}+kM_{D1}(L_{q}+L_{e})i_{q}\omega+k^{2}M_{D1}M_{Q}i_{Q}\omega

\displaystyle-\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}kM_{D1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+M_{R1}v_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{i}_{q}=L_{q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})i_{d}\omega+kM_{F}L_{q1}i_{F}\omega+kM_{D}L_{q1}i_{D}\omega-L_{q1}(r+R_{e})i_{q}+kM_{Q1}r_{Q}i_{Q}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}L_{q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{i}_{Q}=-kM_{Q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})i_{d}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{F}i_{F}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{D}i_{D}\omega+kM_{Q1}(r+R_{e})i_{q}-L_{Q1}r_{Q}i_{Q}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}kM_{Q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

Dividing both LHS and RHS of
Equation 2.37
by
\sqrt{3}
we get

\displaystyle\frac{\dot{i}_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}=-L_{d1}(r+R_{e})\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}+kM_{F1}r_{F}\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}+kM_{D1}r_{D}\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}-(L_{q}+L_{e})L_{d1}\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega-kM_{Q}L_{d1}\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega

(2.38)

\displaystyle+V_{\infty}L_{d1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)-kM_{F1}\frac{v_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}

\displaystyle\frac{\dot{i}_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}=kM_{F1}(r+R_{e})\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}-L_{F1}r_{F}\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}-M_{R1}r_{D}\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}+kM_{F1}(L_{q}+L_{e})\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega+k^{2}M_{F1}M_{Q}\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega

\displaystyle\-\displaystyle V_{\infty}kM_{F1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+L_{F1}\frac{v_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}

\displaystyle\frac{\dot{i}_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}=kM_{D1}(r+R_{e})\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}-M_{R1}r_{F}\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}-L_{D1}r_{D}\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}+kM_{D1}(L_{q}+L_{e})\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega+k^{2}M_{D1}M_{Q}\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega

\displaystyle-V_{\infty}kM_{D1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+M_{R1}\frac{v_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}

\displaystyle\frac{\dot{i}_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}=L_{q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega+kM_{F}L_{q1}\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega+kM_{D}L_{q1}\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega-L_{q1}(r+R_{e})\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}+kM_{Q1}r_{Q}\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}

\displaystyle-V_{\infty}L_{q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\frac{\dot{i}_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}=-kM_{Q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{F}\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{D}\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}\omega+kM_{Q1}(r+R_{e})\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}-L_{Q1}r_{Q}\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}

\displaystyle+V_{\infty}kM_{Q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

Converting the state variables
i_{d}
,
i_{F}
,
i_{D}
,
i_{q}
,
i_{Q}
, and control input
v_{F}
to their corresponding RMS quantities

I_{d}
,
I_{F}
,
I_{D}
,
I_{q}
,
I_{Q}
, and
V_{F}
by substituting
\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{d}
,
\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{F}
,
\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{D}
,
\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{q}
,
\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{Q}
, and
\frac{v_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}=V_{F}
in

Equation 2.38
we get

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{d}=-L_{d1}(r+R_{e})I_{d}+kM_{F1}r_{F}I_{F}+kM_{D1}r_{D}I_{D}-(L_{q}+L_{e})L_{d1}I_{q}\omega-kM_{Q}L_{d1}I_{Q}\omega+V_{\infty}L_{d1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

(2.39)

\displaystyle-kM_{F1}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{F}=kM_{F1}(r+R_{e})I_{d}-L_{F1}r_{F}I_{F}-M_{R1}r_{D}I_{D}+kM_{F1}(L_{q}+L_{e})I_{q}\omega+k^{2}M_{F1}M_{Q}I_{Q}\omega-V_{\infty}kM_{F1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+L_{F1}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{D}=kM_{D1}(r+R_{e})I_{d}-M_{R1}r_{F}I_{F}-L_{D1}r_{D}I_{D}+kM_{D1}(L_{q}+L_{e})I_{q}\omega+k^{2}M_{D1}M_{Q}I_{Q}\omega-V_{\infty}kM_{D1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+M_{R1}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{q}=L_{q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})I_{d}\omega+kM_{F}L_{q1}I_{F}\omega+kM_{D}L_{q1}I_{D}\omega-L_{q1}(r+R_{e})I_{q}+kM_{Q1}r_{Q}I_{Q}-V_{\infty}L_{q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{Q}=-kM_{Q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})I_{d}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{F}I_{F}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{D}I_{D}\omega+kM_{Q1}(r+R_{e})I_{q}-L_{Q1}r_{Q}I_{Q}

\displaystyle+V_{\infty}kM_{Q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

Equation 2.35
can be written as

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}\bigg(L_{d}\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}+kM_{F}\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}+kM_{D}\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}-L_{q}\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}-kM_{Q}\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}\bigg)-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}D\omega+\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}T_{m}

(2.40)

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}=\omega-1

Substituting
\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{d}
,
\frac{i_{F}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{F}
,
\frac{i_{D}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{D}
,
\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{q}
,
\frac{i_{Q}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{Q}
in
Equation 2.40

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}\bigg((L_{d}-L_{q})I_{d}I_{q}+kM_{F}I_{F}I_{q}+kM_{D}I_{D}I_{q}-kM_{Q}I_{d}I_{Q}\bigg)-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}D\omega+\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}T_{m}

(2.41)

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}=\omega-1

Equation 2.39
and

Equation 2.41
can be combined to get the truth model of the synchronous generator

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{d}=-L_{d1}(r+R_{e})I_{d}+kM_{F1}r_{F}I_{F}+kM_{D1}r_{D}I_{D}-(L_{q}+L_{e})L_{d1}I_{q}\omega-kM_{Q}L_{d1}I_{Q}\omega+V_{\infty}L_{d1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

(2.42)

\displaystyle-kM_{F1}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{F}=kM_{F1}(r+R_{e})I_{d}-L_{F1}r_{F}I_{F}-M_{R1}r_{D}I_{D}+kM_{F1}(L_{q}+L_{e})I_{q}\omega+k^{2}M_{F1}M_{Q}I_{Q}\omega-V_{\infty}kM_{F1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+L_{F1}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{D}=kM_{D1}(r+R_{e})I_{d}-M_{R1}r_{F}I_{F}-L_{D1}r_{D}I_{D}+kM_{D1}(L_{q}+L_{e})I_{q}\omega+k^{2}M_{D1}M_{Q}I_{Q}\omega-V_{\infty}kM_{D1}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+M_{R1}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{q}=L_{q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})I_{d}\omega+kM_{F}L_{q1}I_{F}\omega+kM_{D}L_{q1}I_{D}\omega-L_{q1}(r+R_{e})I_{q}+kM_{Q1}r_{Q}I_{Q}-V_{\infty}L_{q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{Q}=-kM_{Q1}(L_{d}+L_{e})I_{d}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{F}I_{F}\omega-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{D}I_{D}\omega+kM_{Q1}(r+R_{e})I_{q}-L_{Q1}r_{Q}I_{Q}

\displaystyle+V_{\infty}kM_{Q1}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}\bigg((L_{d}-L_{q})I_{d}I_{q}+kM_{F}I_{F}I_{q}+kM_{D}I_{D}I_{q}-kM_{Q}I_{d}I_{Q}\bigg)-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}D\omega+\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}T_{m}

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}=\omega-1

For simplification of the above expression let us denote:

\displaystyle F_{11}=-L_{d1}(r+R_{e}),\ \ F_{12}=kM_{F1}r_{F},\ \ F_{13}=kM_{D1}r_{D},\ \ F_{14}=-(L_{q}+L_{e})L_{d1},\ \ F_{15}=-kM_{Q}L_{d1},

(2.43)

\displaystyle F_{16}=V_{\infty}L_{d1},\ \ G_{11}=-kM_{F1},\ \ F_{21}=kM_{F1}(r+R_{e}),\ \ F_{22}=-L_{F1}r_{F},\ \ F_{23}=-M_{R1}r_{D},

\displaystyle F_{24}=kM_{F1}(L_{q}+L_{e}),\ \ F_{25}=k^{2}M_{F1}M_{Q},\ \ F_{26}=-V_{\infty}kM_{F1},\ \ G_{21}=L_{F1},\ \ F_{31}=kM_{D1}(r+R_{e}),

\displaystyle F_{32}=-M_{R1}r_{F},\ \ F_{33}=-L_{D1}r_{D},\ \ F_{34}=kM_{D1}(L_{q}+L_{e}),\ \ F_{35}=k^{2}M_{D1}M_{Q},\ \ F_{36}=-V_{\infty}kM_{D1},

\displaystyle G_{31}=M_{R1},\ \ F_{41}=L_{q1}(L_{d}+L_{e}),\ \ F_{42}=kM_{F}L_{q1},\ \ F_{43}=kM_{D}L_{q1},\ \ F_{44}=-L_{q1}(r+R_{e}),

\displaystyle F_{45}=kM_{Q1}r_{Q},\ \ F_{46}=-V_{\infty}L_{q1},\ \ F_{51}=-kM_{Q1}(L_{d}+L_{e}),\ \ F_{52}=-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{F},\ \ F_{53}=-k^{2}M_{Q1}M_{D},

\displaystyle F_{54}=kM_{Q1}(r+R_{e}),\ \ F_{55}=-L_{Q1}r_{Q},\ \ F_{56}=V_{\infty}kM_{Q1},\ \ F_{61}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}(L_{d}-L_{q}),

\displaystyle F_{62}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}kM_{F},\ \ F_{63}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}kM_{D},\ \ F_{64}=\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}kM_{Q},\ \ F_{65}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}D.

Thus, the
7^{th}
order truth model of the synchronous generator connected to an infinite bus in per unit can be written in the
nonlinear state-space form

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{d}=F_{11}I_{d}+F_{12}I_{F}+F_{13}I_{D}+F_{14}I_{q}\omega+F_{15}I_{Q}\omega+F_{16}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+G_{11}V_{F}

(2.44)

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{F}=F_{21}I_{d}+F_{22}I_{F}+F_{23}I_{D}+F_{24}I_{q}\omega+F_{25}I_{Q}\omega+F_{26}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+G_{21}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{D}=F_{31}I_{d}+F_{32}I_{F}+F_{33}I_{D}+F_{34}I_{q}\omega+F_{35}I_{Q}\omega+F_{36}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+G_{31}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{q}=F_{41}I_{d}\omega+F_{42}I_{F}\omega+F_{43}I_{D}\omega+F_{44}I_{q}+F_{45}I_{Q}+F_{46}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{Q}=F_{51}I_{d}\omega+F_{52}I_{F}\omega+F_{53}I_{D}\omega+F_{54}I_{q}+F_{55}I_{Q}+F_{56}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=F_{61}I_{d}I_{q}+F_{62}I_{F}I_{q}+F_{63}I_{D}I_{q}+F_{64}I_{d}I_{Q}+F_{65}\omega+F_{66}T_{m}

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}=\omega-1

2.5
Model of the Turbine-Governor System

In this section, we present the dynamics of the turbine-governor system. For the sake of simplicity
we assume a linear model of the turbine-governor system
[
9
]
.

•

Turbine dynamics: The dynamics of the turbine are modeled by

\dot{P}_{m}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{T}}P_{m}+\frac{K_{T}}{\tau_{T}}G_{V}

(2.45)

where
P_{m}
is the mechanical power output of the turbine,
G_{V}
is the gate opening of the turbine,

\tau_{T}
is the time constant of the turbine, and
K_{T}
is the gain of the turbine.
As done in
Equation 2.28
we have the per unit mechanical power numerically
equal to the per unit mechanical torque, i.e.
P_{m(p.u.)}=T_{m(p.u.)}
.
Therefore, the per unit turbine dynamics are

\dot{T}_{m(p.u.)}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{T}}T_{m(p.u.)}+\frac{K_{T}}{\tau_{T}}G_{V(p.u.)}

(2.46)

•

Governor dynamics: The dynamics of the governor in per unit are

\dot{G}_{V(p.u.)}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{G}}G_{V(p.u.)}+\frac{K_{G}}{\tau_{G}}\bigg(u_{T}-\frac{\omega_{(p.u.)}}{R_{T}}\bigg)

(2.47)

where
u_{T}
is the turbine valve control,
\tau_{G}
is the time constant of the speed governor,
K_{G}
is the gain
of the speed governor, and
R_{T}
is the regulation constant in per unit.

Parameters of the turbine-governor system are

K_{T}=1,\ \ K_{G}=1,\ \ \tau_{T}=0.5,\ \ \tau_{G}=0.2,\ \ R_{T}=20\\

(2.48)

2.6
Truth model of the combined Synchronous Generator and Turbine-Governor System connected to an infinite bus

In this section, we present the truth model of the combined synchronous generator and turbine-governor system connected
to an infinite bus. This model consists of
7
nonlinear differential equations of the synchronous generator and
2

linear differential equations of the turbine-governor system. Thus, the combined system consists of
9

differential equations. Combining
Equation 2.44
,
Equation 2.46
, and
Equation 2.47
the
truth model of the combined
synchronous generator and turbine-governor system connected to an infinite bus, can be written as

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{d}=F_{11}I_{d}+F_{12}I_{F}+F_{13}I_{D}+F_{14}I_{q}\omega+F_{15}I_{Q}\omega+F_{16}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+G_{11}V_{F}

(2.49)

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{F}=F_{21}I_{d}+F_{22}I_{F}+F_{23}I_{D}+F_{24}I_{q}\omega+F_{25}I_{Q}\omega+F_{26}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+G_{21}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{D}=F_{31}I_{d}+F_{32}I_{F}+F_{33}I_{D}+F_{34}I_{q}\omega+F_{35}I_{Q}\omega+F_{36}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+G_{31}V_{F}

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{q}=F_{41}I_{d}\omega+F_{42}I_{F}\omega+F_{43}I_{D}\omega+F_{44}I_{q}+F_{45}I_{Q}+F_{46}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{Q}=F_{51}I_{d}\omega+F_{52}I_{F}\omega+F_{53}I_{D}\omega+F_{54}I_{q}+F_{55}I_{Q}+F_{56}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=F_{61}I_{d}I_{q}+F_{62}I_{F}I_{q}+F_{63}I_{D}I_{q}+F_{64}I_{d}I_{Q}+F_{65}\omega+F_{66}T_{m}

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}=\omega-1

\displaystyle\dot{T}_{m}=F_{81}T_{m}+F_{82}G_{V}

\displaystyle\dot{G}_{V}=F_{91}\omega+F_{92}G_{V}+G_{92}u_{T}

where
F_{81}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{T}}
,
F_{82}=\frac{K_{T}}{\tau_{T}}
,
F_{91}=-\frac{K_{G}}{\tau_{G}R_{T}}
,
F_{92}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{G}}
,

G_{92}=\frac{K_{G}}{\tau_{G}}
.
Let
x=[I_{d},I_{F},I_{D},I_{q},I_{Q},\omega,\delta,T_{m},G_{V}]^{\mathrm{T}}
be the vector of state variables,

u=[V_{F},u_{T}]^{\mathrm{T}}
the vector of control inputs, and
y=[V_{t},\omega]^{\mathrm{T}}
the vector of outputs, then
Equation 2.49

can be written in the usual state-space form

\displaystyle\dot{x}

\displaystyle=F(x)+G(x)u

(2.50)

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=h(x)

where

\displaystyle F(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}F_{11}x_{1}+F_{12}x_{2}+F_{13}x_{3}+F_{14}x_{4}x_{6}+F_{15}x_{5}x_{6}+F_{16}\sin(x_{7}-\alpha)\\
F_{21}x_{1}+F_{22}x_{2}+F_{23}x_{3}+F_{24}x_{4}x_{6}+F_{25}x_{5}x_{6}+F_{26}\sin(x_{7}-\alpha)\\
F_{31}x_{1}+F_{32}x_{2}+F_{33}x_{3}+F_{34}x_{4}x_{6}+F_{35}x_{5}x_{6}+F_{36}\sin(x_{7}-\alpha)\\
F_{41}x_{1}x_{6}+F_{42}x_{2}x_{6}+F_{43}x_{3}x_{6}+F_{44}x_{4}+F_{45}x_{5}+F_{46}\cos(x_{7}-\alpha)\\
F_{51}x_{1}x_{6}+F_{52}x_{2}x_{6}+F_{53}x_{3}x_{6}+F_{54}x_{4}+F_{55}x_{5}+F_{56}\cos(x_{7}-\alpha)\\
F_{61}x_{1}x_{4}+F_{62}x_{2}x_{4}+F_{63}x_{3}x_{4}+F_{64}x_{1}x_{5}+F_{65}x_{6}+F_{66}x_{8}\\
x_{6}-1\\
F_{81}x_{8}+F_{82}x_{9}\\
F_{91}x_{6}+F_{92}x_{9}\\
\end{pmatrix}

(2.51)

\displaystyle G(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}G_{11}&0\\
G_{21}&0\\
G_{31}&0\\
0&0\\
0&0\\
0&0\\
0&G_{92}\end{pmatrix}

2.7
Derivation of the Output Generator Terminal Voltage for the Truth Model

The synchronous generator and turbine-governor system connected to an infinite bus is a MIMO system with two inputs: excitation
field voltage
V_{F}
and turbine valve control
u_{T}
, i.e.
u=[u_{1},u_{2}]^{\mathrm{T}}=[V_{F},u_{T}]^{\mathrm{T}}
, and two
regulated outputs: generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
and rotor angle
\delta
, i.e.

y=[y_{1},y_{2}]^{\mathrm{T}}=[V_{t},\delta]^{\mathrm{T}}
. Since the rotor angle is difficult to measure in a practical system,
we use the angular frequency
\omega
instead of the rotor angle as the second output
in all our work, as the frequency
\omega
can be easily measured. In this section we derive an expression for the generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
.
From
Equation 2.12
we have

\displaystyle v_{d}=R_{e}i_{d}+L_{e}\dot{i}_{d}+\omega L_{e}i_{q}-\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

(2.52)

\displaystyle v_{q}=R_{e}i_{q}+L_{e}\dot{i}_{q}-\omega L_{e}i_{d}+\sqrt{3}V_{\infty}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

Dividing
Equation 2.52
by
\sqrt{3}
and substituting
\frac{v_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}=V_{d}
,
\frac{v_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}=V_{q}
,

\frac{i_{d}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{d}
, and
\frac{i_{q}}{\sqrt{3}}=I_{q}
, where
V_{d}
,
V_{q}
,
I_{d}
, and
I_{q}
are the corresponding RMS
values, we get

\displaystyle V_{d}=R_{e}I_{d}+L_{e}\dot{I}_{d}+\omega L_{e}I_{q}-V_{\infty}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

(2.53)

\displaystyle V_{q}=R_{e}I_{q}+L_{e}\dot{I}_{q}-\omega L_{e}I_{d}+V_{\infty}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

Substituting
\dot{I}_{d}
and
\dot{I}_{q}
from
Equation 2.49
in
Equation 2.53

\displaystyle V_{d}

\displaystyle=R_{e}I_{d}+L_{e}(F_{11}I_{d}+F_{12}I_{F}+F_{13}I_{D}+F_{14}I_{q}\omega+F_{15}I_{Q}\omega+F_{16}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+G_{11}v_{F})+\omega L_{e}I_{q}

(2.54)

\displaystyle-V_{\infty}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle V_{q}

\displaystyle=R_{e}I_{q}+L_{e}(F_{41}I_{d}\omega+F_{42}I_{F}\omega+F_{43}I_{D}\omega+F_{44}I_{q}+F_{45}I_{Q}+F_{46}\cos(\delta-\alpha))-\omega L_{e}I_{d}+V_{\infty}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

Simplifying and rearranging
Equation 2.54
we get

\displaystyle V_{d}=(R_{e}+L_{e}F_{11})I_{d}+L_{e}F_{12}I_{F}+L_{e}F_{13}I_{D}+(L_{e}F_{14}+L_{e})I_{q}\omega+L_{e}F_{15}I_{Q}\omega+(L_{e}F_{16}-V_{\infty})\sin(\delta-\alpha)

(2.55)

\displaystyle+L_{e}G_{11}V_{F}

\displaystyle V_{q}=(L_{e}F_{41}-L_{e})I_{d}\omega+L_{e}F_{42}I_{F}\omega+L_{e}F_{43}I_{D}\omega+(R_{e}+L_{e}F_{44})I_{q}+L_{e}F_{45}I_{Q}+(L_{e}F_{46}+V_{\infty})\cos(\delta-\alpha)

For simplification of
Equation 2.55
let us denote
R_{e}+L_{e}F_{11}=y_{11}
,
L_{e}F_{12}=y_{12}
,

L_{e}F_{13}=y_{13}
,
L_{e}F_{14}+L_{e}=y_{14}
,
L_{e}F_{15}=y_{15}
,
L_{e}F_{16}-V_{\infty}=y_{16}
,
L_{e}G_{11}=i_{11}
,
L_{e}F_{41}-L_{e}=y_{21}
,
L_{e}F_{42}=y_{22}
,
L_{e}F_{43}=y_{23}
,
R_{e}+L_{e}F_{44}=y_{24}
,
L_{e}F_{45}=y_{25}
, and

L_{e}F_{46}+V_{\infty}=y_{26}
.
Thus,
Equation 2.55
can be simplified to

\displaystyle V_{d}=y_{11}I_{d}+y_{12}I_{F}+y_{13}I_{D}+y_{14}I_{q}\omega+y_{15}I_{Q}\omega+y_{16}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+i_{11}V_{F}

(2.56)

\displaystyle V_{q}=y_{21}I_{d}\omega+y_{22}I_{F}\omega+y_{23}I_{D}\omega+y_{24}I_{q}+y_{25}I_{Q}+y_{26}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

The generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
is computed as

V_{t}=\sqrt{V^{2}_{d}+V^{2}_{q}}

(2.57)

where
V_{d}
and
V_{q}
are as given in
Equation 2.55
.
The output generator terminal voltage,
y_{1}=V_{t}
, as a function of the states
x
, and control inputs
u
, is

\displaystyle V_{d}=y_{11}x_{1}+y_{12}x_{2}+y_{13}x_{3}+y_{14}x_{4}x_{6}+y_{15}x_{5}x_{6}+y_{16}\sin(x_{7}-\alpha)+i_{11}u_{1}

(2.58)

\displaystyle V_{q}=y_{21}x_{1}x_{6}+y_{22}x_{2}x_{6}+y_{23}x_{3}x_{6}+y_{24}x_{4}+y_{25}x_{5}+y_{26}\cos(x_{7}-\alpha)

\displaystyle y_{1}=V_{t}=\sqrt{V^{2}_{d}+V^{2}_{q}}

Therefore, the output equation is given by

y=h(x)=\begin{bmatrix}V_{t}\\
x_{6}\end{bmatrix}

(2.59)

where
V_{t}
is as given in
Equation 2.57
.

3
The Reduced Order Simplified Model

A detailed derivation of the reduced order simplified model of a synchronous generator connected to an infinite bus
is given in the
Appendix
.

3.1
Reduced order model of the combined Synchronous Generator and Turbine-Governor system connected to an infinite bus

The final system equations for the reduced order simplified model of the synchronous generator are summarized below

\displaystyle\dot{E}^{\prime}_{q}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}(E_{FD}-E^{\prime}_{q}+(L_{d}-L^{\prime}_{d})I_{d})

(3.1)

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}[T_{m}-D\omega-(E^{\prime}_{q}I_{q}-(L_{q}-L^{\prime}_{d})I_{d}I_{q})]

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}

\displaystyle=\omega-1

where
E^{\prime}_{d}
is the
d
axis voltage behind the transient reactance
L^{\prime}_{q}
, and
E^{\prime}_{q}
is the
q
axis voltage behind the transient reactance
L^{\prime}_{d}
, where
L^{\prime}_{d}=L_{d}-\frac{(kM_{F})^{2}}{L_{F}}
.
\tau^{\prime}_{d0}
is the
d
axis transient open circuit time constant and is
given by the relation
\tau^{\prime}_{d0}=\frac{L_{F}}{r_{F}}
.
E_{FD}
is the excitation field emf, and
\tau_{j}=2H\omega_{R}
. Also
I_{d}
and
I_{q}
are the direct axis and quadrature axis currents respectively.
E^{\prime}_{d}
is given by an algebraic constraint

E^{\prime}_{d}=-(L_{q}-L^{\prime}_{q})I_{q}

(3.2)

Note that all the variables in the third axis model of
Equation 3.1
are RMS quantities.

By applying Kirchhoff’s voltage law (KVL) to the
d
axis and
q
axis stator circuits, the
d
axis and
q
axis stator voltage equations of a synchronous generator in per unit are

\displaystyle V_{d}

\displaystyle=-rI_{d}-L^{\prime}_{q}I_{q}+E^{\prime}_{d}

(3.3)

\displaystyle V_{q}

\displaystyle=-rI_{q}+L^{\prime}_{d}I_{d}+E^{\prime}_{q}

On substituting
E^{\prime}_{d}
as given in
Equation 3.2
in
Equation 3.3
we get

\displaystyle V_{d}

\displaystyle=-rI_{d}-L_{q}I_{q}

(3.4)

\displaystyle V_{q}

\displaystyle=-rI_{q}+L^{\prime}_{d}I_{d}+E^{\prime}_{q}

By applying KVL to a synchronous generator connected to an infinite bus the stator voltage equations can be written as

\displaystyle V_{\infty d}

\displaystyle=V_{d}-R_{e}I_{d}-L_{e}I_{q}

(3.5)

\displaystyle=-(r+R_{e})I_{d}-(L_{q}+L_{e})I_{q}

\displaystyle V_{\infty q}

\displaystyle=V_{q}-R_{e}I_{q}+L_{e}I_{d}

\displaystyle=-(r+R_{e})I_{q}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})I_{d}+E^{\prime}_{q}

where
V_{\infty d}
and
V_{\infty d}
are direct axis and quadrature axis infinite bus voltages respectively,
R_{e}
is the
resistance and
L_{e}
is the inductance of the infinite bus.
We now solve the two simultaneous equations given in
Equation 3.5
to determine the two unknowns
I_{d}
and
I_{q}
.
On dividing
V_{\infty d}
in
Equation 3.5
by
-(L_{q}+L_{e})

\frac{V_{\infty d}}{-(L_{q}+L_{e})}=\frac{-(r+R_{e})}{-(L_{q}+L_{e})}I_{d}+I_{q}

(3.6)

Similarly dividing
V_{\infty q}
in
Equation 3.5
by
r+R_{e}

\frac{V_{\infty q}}{(r+R_{e})}=-I_{q}+\frac{(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})}I_{d}+\frac{E^{\prime}_{q}}{(r+R_{e})}

(3.7)

Now we add
Equation 3.6
and
Equation 3.7
and compute
I_{d}

I_{d}=\frac{-(E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{\infty q})(L_{q}+L_{e})-V_{\infty d}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}

(3.8)

I_{q}
is determined in a similar fashion.
Dividing
V_{\infty d}
in
Equation 3.5
by
(r+R_{e})

\frac{V_{\infty d}}{(r+R_{e})}=-I_{d}-\frac{(L_{q}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})}I_{q}

(3.9)

Also dividing
V_{\infty q}
in
Equation 3.5
by
L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e}

\frac{V_{\infty q}}{(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}=-\frac{(r+R_{e})}{(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}I_{q}+I_{d}+\frac{E^{\prime}_{q}}{(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}

(3.10)

Now we add
Equation 3.9
and
Equation 3.10
and compute
I_{q}

I_{q}=\frac{(E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{\infty q})(r+R_{e})-V_{\infty d}(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}

(3.11)

We substitute
I_{d}
and
I_{q}
as given in
Equation 3.8
and
Equation 3.11

in the reduced order simplified model of the synchronous generator as given in
Equation 3.1
to get

\dot{E}^{\prime}_{q}=\frac{1}{\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}\bigg(E_{FD}-E^{\prime}_{q}+(L_{d}-L^{\prime}_{d})\bigg(\frac{-(E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{\infty q})(L_{q}+L_{e})-V_{\infty d}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\bigg)\bigg)

(3.12)

For simplification of the above equation we make the following substitutions:

L_{q}+L_{e}=L_{1},r+R_{e}=R_{1},L_{d}-L^{\prime}_{d}=L_{2}
and
(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})=M_{1}

Thus,
Equation 3.12
can be rewritten as

\dot{E}^{\prime}_{q}=\frac{1}{\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}\bigg(E_{FD}-E^{\prime}_{q}-\frac{L_{2}L_{1}}{M_{1}}E^{\prime}_{q}+\frac{L_{2}L_{1}}{M_{1}}V_{\infty q}-\frac{L_{2}R_{1}}{M_{1}}V_{\infty d}\bigg)

(3.13)

Substituting
V_{\infty d}=-V_{\infty}\sin(\delta-\alpha)
and
V_{\infty q}=V_{\infty}\cos(\delta-\alpha)
in

Equation 3.13
and rearranging it we get

\dot{E}^{\prime}_{q}=\frac{-(1+\frac{L_{2}L_{1}}{M_{1}})}{\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}E^{\prime}_{q}+\frac{L_{2}L_{1}V_{\infty}}{M_{1}\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+\frac{L_{2}R_{1}V_{\infty}}{M_{1}\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+\frac{1}{\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}E_{FD}

(3.14)

Let us denote
\frac{-(1+\frac{L_{2}L_{1}}{M_{1}})}{\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}=f_{11}
,
\frac{L_{2}L_{1}V_{\infty}}{M_{1}\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}=f_{12}
,

\frac{L_{2}R_{1}V_{\infty}}{M_{1}\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}=f_{13}
and
\frac{1}{\tau^{\prime}_{d0}}=g_{11}
. Thus,
Equation 3.14

can be rewritten as

\dot{E}^{\prime}_{q}=f_{11}E^{\prime}_{q}+f_{12}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+f_{13}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+g_{11}E_{FD}

(3.15)

Substituting
I_{d}
and
I_{q}
in the equation for
\dot{\omega}
in the reduced order simplified model of
the synchronous generator as given in
Equation 3.1
we get

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}\bigg[T_{m}-D\omega-\bigg(E^{\prime}_{q}\bigg(\frac{(E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{\infty q})(r+R_{e})-V_{\infty d}(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\bigg)

(3.16)

\displaystyle-(L_{q}-L^{\prime}_{d})\bigg(\frac{-(E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{\infty q})(L_{q}+L_{e})-V_{\infty d}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{(E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{\infty q})(r+R_{e})-V_{\infty d}(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg]

Let us denote
L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e}=L_{3}
and
L_{q}-L^{\prime}_{d}=L_{4}
. Substituting
V_{\infty d}=-V_{\infty}\sin(\delta-\alpha)
and
V_{\infty q}=V_{\infty}\cos(\delta-\alpha)
in
Equation 3.16
and on simplifying we get

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{R_{1}}{M_{1}\tau_{j}}+\frac{L_{4}L_{1}R_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)E^{\prime 2}_{q}+\bigg(\frac{R_{1}}{M_{1}\tau_{j}}+\frac{2L_{4}L_{1}R_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)V_{\infty}E^{\prime}_{q}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

(3.17)

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{L_{3}}{M_{1}\tau_{j}}+\frac{L_{4}L_{1}L_{3}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}-\frac{L_{4}R^{2}_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)V_{\infty}E^{\prime}_{q}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{L_{4}R^{2}_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}-\frac{L_{4}L_{1}L_{3}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)V^{2}_{\infty}\sin(\delta-\alpha)\cos(\delta-\alpha)-\bigg(\frac{L_{4}L_{1}R_{1}V^{2}_{\infty}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)\cos^{2}(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\frac{L_{4}L_{3}R_{1}V^{2}_{\infty}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)\sin^{2}(\delta-\alpha)-\frac{D}{\tau_{j}}\omega+\frac{1}{\tau_{j}}T_{m}

Let us denote

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{R_{1}}{M_{1}\tau_{j}}+\frac{L_{4}L_{1}R_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)=f_{21},\ \bigg(\frac{R_{1}}{M_{1}\tau_{j}}+\frac{2L_{4}L_{1}R_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)V_{\infty}=f_{22},

(3.18)

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{L_{3}}{M_{1}\tau_{j}}+\frac{L_{4}L_{1}L_{3}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}-\frac{L_{4}R^{2}_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)V_{\infty}=f_{23},\ -\bigg(\frac{L_{4}R^{2}_{1}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}-\frac{L_{4}L_{1}L_{3}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)V^{2}_{\infty}=f_{24},

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{L_{4}L_{1}R_{1}V^{2}_{\infty}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)=f_{25},\ \bigg(\frac{L_{4}L_{3}R_{1}V^{2}_{\infty}}{M^{2}_{1}\tau_{j}}\bigg)=f_{26},\ -\frac{D}{\tau_{j}}=f_{27},\ \mathrm{and}\ \frac{1}{\tau_{j}}=f_{28}

Thus,
Equation 3.17
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=

\displaystyle f_{21}E^{\prime 2}_{q}+f_{22}E^{\prime}_{q}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+f_{23}E^{\prime}_{q}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+f_{24}\sin(\delta-\alpha)\cos(\delta-\alpha)

(3.19)

\displaystyle+f_{25}\cos^{2}(\delta-\alpha)+f_{26}\sin^{2}(\delta-\alpha)+f_{27}\omega+f_{28}T_{m}

We can express the reduced order nonlinear model of the synchronous generator connected to an infinite bus
in the usual state space form

\displaystyle\dot{E}^{\prime}_{q}=f_{11}E^{\prime}_{q}+f_{12}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+f_{13}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+g_{11}E_{FD}

(3.20)

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=f_{21}E^{\prime 2}_{q}+f_{22}E^{\prime}_{q}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+f_{23}E^{\prime}_{q}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+f_{24}\sin(\delta-\alpha)\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+f_{25}\cos^{2}(\delta-\alpha)+f_{26}\sin^{2}(\delta-\alpha)+f_{27}\omega+f_{28}T_{m}

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}=\omega-1

For the reduced order generator-turbine system we use the same turbine-governor model that we used for the truth model.
The model of the turbine-governor system as given in
Equation 2.46
and
Equation 2.47
in per unit is

\displaystyle\dot{T}_{m}=-\frac{1}{\tau_{T}}T_{m}+\frac{K_{T}}{\tau_{T}}G_{V}

(3.21)

\displaystyle\dot{G}_{V}=-\frac{K_{G}}{\tau_{G}R_{T}}\omega-\frac{1}{\tau_{G}}G_{V}+\frac{K_{G}}{\tau_{G}}u_{T}

The reduced order model of the generator-turbine system connected to an infinite bus consists of
3
nonlinear
differential equations of the synchronous generator and
2
linear differential equations of the turbine-governor system.
Thus, the combined system consists of
5

differential equations.
Combining
Equation 3.20
and
Equation 3.21
the reduced order model of the synchronous generator and turbine connected to an infinite bus can be written as

\displaystyle\dot{E}^{\prime}_{q}=f_{11}E^{\prime}_{q}+f_{12}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+f_{13}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+g_{11}E_{FD}

(3.22)

\displaystyle\dot{\omega}=f_{21}E^{\prime 2}_{q}+f_{22}E^{\prime}_{q}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+f_{23}E^{\prime}_{q}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+f_{24}\sin(\delta-\alpha)\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+f_{25}\cos^{2}(\delta-\alpha)+f_{26}\sin^{2}(\delta-\alpha)+f_{27}\omega+f_{28}T_{m}

\displaystyle\dot{\delta}=\omega-1

\displaystyle\dot{T}_{m}=f_{41}T_{m}+f_{42}G_{V}

\displaystyle\dot{G}_{V}=f_{51}\omega+f_{52}G_{V}+g_{55}u_{T}

where,
-\frac{1}{\tau_{T}}=f_{41}
,
\frac{K_{T}}{\tau_{T}}=f_{42}
,
-\frac{K_{G}}{\tau_{G}R_{T}}=f_{51}
,

-\frac{1}{\tau_{G}}=f_{52}
, and
\frac{K_{G}}{\tau_{G}}=g_{55}
.

Let us define the state variables as
x=[E^{\prime}_{q},\omega,\delta,T_{m},G_{V}]^{\mathrm{T}}
, and the two control inputs as
u=[u_{1},u_{2}]^{\mathrm{T}}=[E_{FD},u_{T}]^{\mathrm{T}}
.
We can then express the simplified fifth order nonlinear model of the synchronous generator and turbine connected
to an infinite bus as

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{1}

\displaystyle=f_{11}x_{1}+f_{12}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{13}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)+g_{11}u_{1}

(3.23)

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{2}

\displaystyle=f_{21}x^{2}_{1}+f_{22}x_{1}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{23}x_{1}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{24}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)

\displaystyle+f_{25}\cos^{2}(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{26}\sin^{2}(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{27}x_{2}+f_{28}x_{4}

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{3}

\displaystyle=x_{2}-1

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{4}

\displaystyle=f_{41}x_{4}+f_{42}x_{5}

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{5}

\displaystyle=f_{51}x_{2}+f_{52}x_{5}+g_{55}u_{2}

Thus, we can put the simplified fifth order nonlinear model of the synchronous generator and turbine
connected to an infinite bus in the usual state-space
form

\displaystyle\dot{x}

\displaystyle=f(x)+g(x)u

(3.24)

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=h(x)

where

\displaystyle f(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}f_{11}x_{1}+f_{12}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{13}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)\\
f_{21}x^{2}_{1}+f_{22}x_{1}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{23}x_{1}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{24}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)\cdot\cdot\cdot\\
\cdot\cdot\cdot+f_{25}\cos^{2}(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{26}\sin^{2}(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{27}x_{2}+f_{28}x_{4}\\
x_{2}-1\\
f_{41}x_{4}+f_{42}x_{5}\\
f_{51}x_{2}+f_{52}x_{5}\end{pmatrix}

(3.25)

\displaystyle g(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}g_{11}&0\\
0&0\\
0&0\\
0&0\\
0&g_{55}\end{pmatrix}

In
Equation 3.24
and
Equation 3.25
, the state variables are
x=[E^{\prime}_{q},\omega,\delta,T_{m},G_{V}]^{\mathrm{T}}
, the two control inputs are
u=[E_{FD},u_{T}]^{\mathrm{T}}
, and the two regulated outputs are
y=[V_{t},\omega]^{\mathrm{T}}
.
The expression for the generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
will be derived in the next subsection.

3.2
Derivation of the Output Generator Terminal Voltage for the Reduced Order Model

The reduced order model of the synchronous generator and turbine connected to an infinite bus is a MIMO system with two inputs:
excitation field EMF
E_{FD}
, and turbine valve control
u_{T}
, and two outputs: generator terminal
voltage
V_{t}
and rotor angle
\delta
.
In this section we derive an expression for the generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
which is the first output of the MIMO system.
From
Equation 3.4
the direct axis and quadrature axis stator voltage equations of a synchronous generator are given by

\displaystyle V_{d}

\displaystyle=-rI_{d}-L_{q}I_{q}

(3.26)

\displaystyle V_{q}

\displaystyle=-rI_{q}+L^{\prime}_{d}I_{d}+E^{\prime}_{q}

Since the stator resistance
r\approx 0
we can write

\displaystyle V_{d}

\displaystyle=-L_{q}I_{q}

(3.27)

\displaystyle V_{q}

\displaystyle=L^{\prime}_{d}I_{d}+E^{\prime}_{q}

Substituting
I_{d}
and
I_{q}
as given in
Equation 3.8
and
Equation 3.11
in
Equation 3.27
we get

\displaystyle V_{d}

\displaystyle=-L_{q}I_{q}

(3.28)

\displaystyle=-\frac{L_{q}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}E^{\prime}_{q}

\displaystyle+\frac{V_{\infty}L_{q}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle-\frac{V_{\infty}L_{q}(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

Let us denote

\displaystyle-\frac{L_{q}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}=V_{d1}

(3.29)

\displaystyle\frac{V_{\infty}L_{q}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}=V_{d2}

\displaystyle-\frac{V_{\infty}L_{q}(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}=V_{d3}

Thus,
Equation 3.28
can be rewritten as

V_{d}=V_{d1}E^{\prime}_{q}+V_{d2}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+V_{d3}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

(3.30)

Now let us derive the expression for
V_{q}

\displaystyle V_{q}

\displaystyle=L^{\prime}_{d}I_{d}+E^{\prime}_{q}

(3.31)

\displaystyle=-\frac{L^{\prime}_{d}(L_{q}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}E^{\prime}_{q}

\displaystyle+\frac{V_{\infty}L^{\prime}_{d}(L_{q}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\cos(\delta-\alpha)

\displaystyle+\frac{V_{\infty}L^{\prime}_{d}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+E^{\prime}_{q}

Let us denote

\displaystyle-\frac{L^{\prime}_{d}(L_{q}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}=V_{q1}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\frac{V_{\infty}L^{\prime}_{d}(L_{q}+L_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}=V_{q2}

\displaystyle\frac{V_{\infty}L^{\prime}_{d}(r+R_{e})}{(r+R_{e})^{2}+(L^{\prime}_{d}+L_{e})(L_{q}+L_{e})}=V_{q3}

Thus,
Equation 3.31
can be rewritten as

V_{q}=V_{q1}E^{\prime}_{q}+V_{q2}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+V_{q3}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+E^{\prime}_{q}

(3.33)

The generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
is given by

V_{t}=\sqrt{V^{2}_{d}+V^{2}_{q}}

(3.34)

The output generator terminal voltage,
y_{1}=V_{t}
, as a function of the states
x
, is

\displaystyle V_{d}=V_{d1}x_{1}+V_{d2}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+V_{d3}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)

(3.35)

\displaystyle V_{q}=V_{q1}x_{1}+V_{q2}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+V_{q3}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)+x_{1}

\displaystyle V_{t}=\sqrt{V^{2}_{d}+V^{2}_{q}}

Thus, the output equation consists of a nonlinear equation for
V_{t}
as given in
Equation 3.34
and
Equation 3.35
, and a simple linear equation for
\omega

y=h(x)=\begin{bmatrix}V_{t}\\
x_{2}\end{bmatrix}

(3.36)

3.3
Linearization of the Reduced Order Model by Taylor series approximation

In this section we linearize the fifth-order nonlinear model of the synchronous generator and turbine
connected to an infinite bus by using the Taylor series approximation about a nominal operating point (
x_{0}
,
u_{0}
).
The operating condition is a steady state equilibrium of the system. The steady state equilibrium condition is attained by the system after all the transients die out or decay to zero. The equilibrium point (
x_{0}
,
u_{0}
) is computed by
solving the differential equation,
\dot{x}=f(x_{0})+g(x_{0})u_{0}=0
.
From
Equation 3.25
we can write

f_{1}(x)=f_{11}x_{1}+f_{12}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{13}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)

(3.37)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{1}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=f_{11}

(3.38)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{2}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{3}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=-f_{12}\sin(x_{30}-\alpha)+f_{13}\cos(x_{30}-\alpha)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{4}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{5}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle f_{2}(x)=

\displaystyle f_{21}x^{2}_{1}+f_{22}x_{1}\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{23}x_{1}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{24}\sin(x_{3}-\alpha)\cos(x_{3}-\alpha)

(3.39)

\displaystyle+f_{25}\cos^{2}(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{26}\sin^{2}(x_{3}-\alpha)+f_{27}x_{2}+f_{28}x_{4}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{1}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=2f_{21}x_{10}+f_{22}\cos(x_{30}-\alpha)+f_{23}\sin(x_{30}-\alpha)

(3.40)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{2}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=f_{27}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{3}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=-f_{22}x_{10}\sin(x_{30}-\alpha)+f_{23}x_{10}\cos(x_{30}-\alpha)+f_{24}\cos 2(x_{30}-\alpha)-f_{25}\sin 2(x_{30}-\alpha)+f_{26}\sin 2(x_{30}-\alpha)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{4}0}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=f_{28}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{5}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

f_{3}(x)=x_{2}-1

(3.41)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{1}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

(3.42)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{2}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{3}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{4}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{5}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

f_{4}(x)=f_{41}x_{4}+f_{42}x_{5}

(3.43)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{1}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

(3.44)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{2}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{3}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{4}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=f_{41}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{5}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=f_{42}

f_{5}(x)=f_{51}x_{2}+f_{52}x_{5}

(3.45)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{1}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

(3.46)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{2}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=f_{51}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{3}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{4}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{5}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=f_{52}

g_{1}(x)=g_{11}u_{1}

(3.47)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{1}}{\partial u_{1}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=g_{11}

(3.48)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{1}}{\partial u_{2}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

g_{2}(x)=0

(3.49)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{2}}{\partial u_{1}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

(3.50)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{2}}{\partial u_{2}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

g_{3}(x)=0

(3.51)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{3}}{\partial u_{1}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

(3.52)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{3}}{\partial u_{2}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

g_{4}(x)=0

(3.53)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{4}}{\partial u_{1}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

(3.54)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{4}}{\partial u_{2}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

g_{5}(x)=g_{55}u_{2}

(3.55)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{5}}{\partial u_{1}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=0

(3.56)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{5}}{\partial u_{2}}\bigg|_{u_{0}}

\displaystyle=g_{55}

Therefore, the linear reduced order model of the synchronous generator and turbine-governor system connected to an infinite bus is

\dot{\mathbf{x}}=A\mathbf{x}+B\mathbf{u}

(3.57)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}^{T}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Delta E^{\prime}_{q}&\Delta\omega&\Delta\delta&\Delta T_{m}&\Delta G_{V}\end{bmatrix}

(3.58)

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Delta E_{FD}\\
\Delta u_{T}\end{bmatrix}

In the above equation
\Delta
is the deviation from the nominal operating condition, i.e.
E^{\prime}_{q}-E^{\prime}_{q0}=\Delta E^{\prime}_{q}
,
\omega-\omega_{0}=\Delta\omega
,
\delta-\delta_{0}=\Delta\delta
,
T_{m}-T_{m0}=\Delta T_{m}
,
G_{V}-G_{V0}=\Delta G_{V}
,
E_{FD}-E_{FD0}=\Delta E_{FD}
,
and
u_{T}-u_{T0}=\Delta u_{T}
, and

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{1}}&\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{2}}&\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{3}}&\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{4}}&\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{5}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{1}}&\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{2}}&\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{3}}&\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{4}}&\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{5}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{1}}&\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{2}}&\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{3}}&\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{4}}&\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial x_{5}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{1}}&\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{2}}&\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{3}}&\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{4}}&\frac{\partial f_{4}}{\partial x_{5}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{1}}&\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{2}}&\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{3}}&\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{4}}&\frac{\partial f_{5}}{\partial x_{5}}\\
\\
\end{bmatrix}_{x_{0}}

(3.59)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}f_{11}&0&\frac{\partial f_{1}}{\partial x_{3}}&0&0\\
\\
\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{1}}&f_{27}&\frac{\partial f_{2}}{\partial x_{3}}&f_{28}&0\\
\\
0&1&0&0&0\\
\\
0&0&0&f_{41}&f_{42}\\
\\
0&f_{51}&0&0&f_{52}\\
\end{bmatrix}_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\partial g_{1}}{\partial u_{1}}&\frac{\partial g_{1}}{\partial u_{2}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial g_{2}}{\partial u_{1}}&\frac{\partial g_{2}}{\partial u_{2}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial g_{3}}{\partial u_{1}}&\frac{\partial g_{3}}{\partial u_{2}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial g_{4}}{\partial u_{1}}&\frac{\partial g_{4}}{\partial u_{2}}\\
\\
\frac{\partial g_{5}}{\partial u_{1}}&\frac{\partial g_{5}}{\partial u_{2}}\\
\\
\end{array}\right]_{u_{0}}

(3.60)

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}g_{11}&0\\
0&0\\
0&0\\
0&0\\
0&g_{55}\\
\end{array}\right]_{u_{0}}

In order to design a linear controller for the linearized model obtained by using Taylor series approximation,
we need to find an expression for the first output
\Delta V_{t}
.
By using Taylor series approximation, we have

V_{t}(x)=V_{t}(x_{0})+\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{t}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})+.....

(3.61)

Where,
x=[E^{\prime}_{q},\omega,\delta,T_{m},G_{V}]^{\mathrm{T}}
, are the states of the reduced order nonlinear model.
Let us denote
V_{t}(x)-V_{t}(x_{0})=\Delta V_{t}
, and
V_{t}(x_{0})=V_{t0}
. By neglecting the second and higher order
derivatives in
Equation 3.61
we have

\Delta V_{t}=\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{t}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})

(3.62)

From
Equation 3.34
and
Equation 3.35
the generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
is given by

V_{t}=\sqrt{V^{2}_{d}+V^{2}_{q}}

(3.63)

where

\displaystyle V_{d}=V_{d1}E^{\prime}_{q}+V_{d2}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+V_{d3}\sin(\delta-\alpha)

(3.64)

\displaystyle V_{q}=V_{q1}E^{\prime}_{q}+V_{q2}\cos(\delta-\alpha)+V_{q3}\sin(\delta-\alpha)+E^{\prime}_{q}

We can write

\Delta V_{t}=\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{t}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})=\bigg(\frac{\partial V_{t}}{\partial V_{d}}\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg)\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})+\bigg(\frac{\partial V_{t}}{\partial V_{q}}\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{q}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg)\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})

(3.65)

Differentiating
Equation 3.63
with respect to
V_{d}
and
V_{q}
respectively

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V_{t}}{\partial V_{d}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}=\frac{V_{d0}}{\sqrt{V^{2}_{d0}+V^{2}_{q0}}}=\frac{V_{d0}}{V_{t0}}

(3.66)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V_{t}}{\partial V_{q}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}=\frac{V_{q0}}{\sqrt{V^{2}_{d0}+V^{2}_{q0}}}=\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}

Also

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})=(E^{\prime}_{q}-E^{\prime}_{q0})\frac{\partial V_{d}}{\partial E^{\prime}_{q}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(\omega-\omega_{0})\frac{\partial V_{d}}{\partial\omega}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(\delta-\delta_{0})\frac{\partial V_{d}}{\partial\delta}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(T_{m}-T_{m0})\frac{\partial V_{d}}{\partial T_{m}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(G_{V}-G_{V0})\frac{\partial V_{d}}{\partial G_{V}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

(3.67)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{q}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})=(E^{\prime}_{q}-E^{\prime}_{q0})\frac{\partial V_{q}}{\partial E^{\prime}_{q}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(\omega-\omega_{0})\frac{\partial V_{q}}{\partial\omega}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(\delta-\delta_{0})\frac{\partial V_{q}}{\partial\delta}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(T_{m}-T_{m0})\frac{\partial V_{q}}{\partial T_{m}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}+(G_{V}-G_{V0})\frac{\partial V_{q}}{\partial G_{V}}\bigg|_{x_{0}}

Let us recall that the deviations of the state variables form their nominal operating condition are given by:

E^{\prime}_{q}-E^{\prime}_{q0}=\Delta E^{\prime}_{q}
,
\omega-\omega_{0}=\Delta\omega
,
\delta-\delta_{0}=\Delta\delta
,

T_{m}-T_{m0}=\Delta T_{m}
,
G_{V}-G_{V0}=\Delta G_{V}
. Simplifying
Equation 3.67
we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})=V_{d1}\Delta E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{d2}\sin(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)\Delta\delta+V_{d3}\cos(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)\Delta\delta

(3.68)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{q}}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x_{0}}(x-x_{0})=V_{q1}\Delta E^{\prime}_{q}-V_{q2}\sin(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)\Delta\delta+V_{q3}\cos(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)\Delta\delta+\triangle E^{\prime}_{q}

Substituting
Equation 3.66
and
Equation 3.68
in
Equation 3.65
and rearranging we get

\displaystyle\Delta V_{t}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{V_{d0}}{V_{t0}}V_{d1}+\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}V_{q1}+\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}\bigg)\Delta E^{\prime}_{q}

(3.69)

\displaystyle+\bigg(-\frac{V_{d0}}{V_{t0}}V_{d2}\sin(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)+\frac{V_{d0}}{V_{t0}}V_{d3}\cos(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)-\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}V_{q2}\sin(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)+\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}V_{q3}\cos(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)\bigg)\Delta\delta

Let us denote

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{V_{d0}}{V_{t0}}V_{d1}+\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}V_{q1}+\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}\bigg)=T_{1}

(3.70)

\displaystyle\bigg(-\frac{V_{d0}}{V_{t0}}V_{d2}\sin(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)+\frac{V_{d0}}{V_{t0}}V_{d3}\cos(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)-\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}V_{q2}\sin(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)+\frac{V_{q0}}{V_{t0}}V_{q3}\cos(\delta_{\circ}-\alpha)\bigg)=T_{2}

Thus,
Equation 3.69
can be simplified to get

\Delta V_{t}=T_{1}\Delta E^{\prime}_{q}+T_{2}\Delta\delta

(3.71)

Thus, the linearized output equation of the reduced order model is given by

\mathbf{y}=C\mathbf{x}

(3.72)

where

\mathbf{y}=\begin{bmatrix}\Delta V_{t}\\
\Delta\omega\end{bmatrix}

(3.73)

and the output matrix
C
is

C=\begin{bmatrix}T_{1}&0&T_{2}&0&0\\
0&1&0&0&0\end{bmatrix}

(3.74)

3.4
Example

In this section we calculate the
(A,B,C)
system matrices of the truth model
and the reduced order model linearized about a given nominal operating point. The power, voltage, and current ratings
as well as the parameters of the synchronous generator
are given in
Section 3.4
, which contains values for an actual synchronous generator
with some quantities, denoted by an asterisk, being estimated for our study
[
1
]
. The power rating of a synchronous generator is equal to the product of the voltage per phase, the current per phase, and the number of phases. It is normally stated in megavolt-amperes (MVA) for large generators. The parameters given in
Section 3.4
are not in the per unit system.
The quantities in
Section 3.4
are converted to the per unit system, which are given in
Section 3.4

[
1
]
.
Section 3.4
gives the parameters of the reduced order nonlinear model in the per unit system.

Before we proceed to the calculation of the system matrices, we briefly explain a basic power system terminology, that is used in
determining a suitable operating condition. Real power (also known as active power) (
P
), measured in watts (W); apparent power (
S
), measured in volt-amperes (VA); and reactive power (
Q
), measured in reactive volt-amperes (var) are
related by the expression,
S=P+jQ
in vector form. If
\phi
is the phase angle between the current and voltage, then the power factor (PF) is equal to the cosine of the angle,
|\cos\phi|
, and
|P|=|S|\cos\phi
and
|Q|=|S|\sin\phi
. Power factors are usually stated as ”leading” or ”lagging” to show the sign of the phase angle
\phi
. If a purely resistive load is connected to a power supply, current and voltage will change polarity in step, the power factor will be unity, and the electrical energy flows in a single direction across the network in each cycle. Inductive loads such as transformers and motors (any type of wound coil) consume reactive power with current waveform lagging the voltage. Capacitive loads such as capacitor banks or buried cable generate reactive power with current phase leading the voltage. Both types of loads will absorb energy during part of the AC cycle, which is stored in the device’s magnetic or electric field, only to return this energy back to the source during the rest of the cycle. Since a majority of the loads
in a power grid are inductive, we assume power factor lagging conditions, where the generator armature current lags the generator
terminal voltage. Also a high power factor is generally desirable in a transmission system to reduce transmission losses and improve voltage regulation at the load. A power factor of
0.85
is assumed, which is within the stable operating limits of a synchronous
generator.

The synchronous generator is connected to an infinite bus through a transmission line having
R_{e}=0.02
p.u., and
L_{e}=0.4
p.u. The infinite bus voltage is
1.0
p.u. The machine loading is given by, real power
P=1.0
p.u. at
0.85
PF lagging conditions. The steady state operating conditions of a synchronous generator turbine system connected to an infinite bus depend on the synchronous generator turbine system parameters, transmission line parameters, and the machine loading. For the synchronous generator with parameters and loading conditions given in
Section 3.4
and
Section 3.4
, a steady state operating point for the truth model is evaluated which is as given in
[
1
]
:

(3.75)

4
Open Loop Input-Output Behavior of the Synchronous Generator and Turbine-Governor System

In this section we observe the open loop input-output behavior of the truth model and the reduced order nonlinear model
of the SMIB for test signals given in
Figure 4.2
and
Figure 4.2
.
The first control input
V_{F}
of the truth model and the first control input
E_{FD}
of the reduced order model are different. As given in

Equation 10.41
the field voltage,
V_{F}
, is
related to excitation field emf,
E_{FD}
, by the following expression

\displaystyle V_{F}=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{r_{F}}{\omega_{R}kM_{F}}\bigg)E_{FD}

(4.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e_{15}E_{FD}

In the above expression,
\omega_{R}=1
p.u., and
e_{15}=(\frac{r_{F}}{\omega_{R}kM_{F}})
.

Figure 4.1:
Plot of
\Gamma_{1}
(Test Signal 1) vs time

Figure 4.2:
Plot of
\Gamma_{2}
(Test Signal 2) vs time

We first test the truth model and the reduced order nonlinear model of the SMIB
for
\Gamma_{1}
(Test signal 1) given in
Figure 4.2
.
In this case the two inputs for the truth
model are,
u=[V_{F},u_{T}]^{\mathrm{T}}=[e_{15}\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{1}]^{\mathrm{T}}
, and the two inputs for the reduced order nonlinear model are,
u=[E_{FD},u_{T}]^{\mathrm{T}}=[\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{1}]^{\mathrm{T}}
.

Figure 4.5
,
Figure 4.5
, and
Figure 4.5

show generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
, rotor angle
\delta
, and frequency
\omega
vs time plots for
\Gamma_{1}
(Test Signal 1) applied to the reduced order nonlinear model. From
Figure 4.5
we can see that after the addition of the fourth step of
\Gamma_{1}
(Test Signal 1) at 600 seconds, the generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
settles to a new steady state value of 0.83 p.u..
From
Figure 4.5
we can see that the rotor angle
\delta
first undergoes a large undershoot, followed by oscillations about 0.1 p.u. after the application of the second incremental step of
\Gamma_{1}
at 200 seconds, which is further
followed by reduced oscillations about 0.3 p.u.
after the application of the third step of
\Gamma_{1}
at 400 seconds, and finally after the application of the fourth and final step at 600 seconds the oscillations almost die out and the rotor angle
\delta
settles to a value of 0.725 p.u..
Figure 4.5
shows that the frequency
\omega
settles
to its steady state value of 1 p.u. after the application of the fourth step at 600 seconds. Like the rotor angle
\delta
, the oscillations of the frequency
\omega
also decrease with the application of each incremental step.

Figure 4.8
,
Figure 4.8
, and
Figure 4.8

show generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
, rotor angle
\delta
, and frequency
\omega
vs time plots for
\Gamma_{1}
(Test Signal 1) applied to the truth model. From
Figure 4.8
we can see that the generator terminal voltage
V_{t}
converges
very slowly to the new steady state value of 0.83 p.u.. From
Figure 4.8
we can see that the rotor angle
\delta
first undergoes a large undershoot after which it oscillates about 0.1 p.u., where the oscillations decay with time.
Figure 4.8
shows that the frequency
\omega
oscillates about its steady state value of 1 p.u.. We observe a
peculiar difference between the performance of the reduced order nonlinear model and the truth model, for
\Gamma_{1}
(Test Signal 1).
The addition of each incremental step of
\Gamma_{1}
after a fixed interval has a distinct effect on the amplitude and damping of the output oscillations for the reduced order nonlinear model, where a rapid decrease in the output oscillations is observed with the application of each step. Thus, we observe transitions in the output response at time instants when incremental steps of
\Gamma_{1}
are applied to the reduced order nonlinear model. However, for the truth model, the effect of adding incremental steps at regular intervals is negligible on the output response, compared to the reduced order nonlinear model. We do not see transitions in the output behavior at time instants when incremental steps of
\Gamma_{1}
are applied to the truth model, and the oscillations decay more
uniformly in this case.

u=[V_{F},u_{T}]^{\mathrm{T}}=[e_{15}\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{2}]^{\mathrm{T}}

oscillates about

after regular

(Test Signal 1). The oscillations decay uniformly with time, and we do not see the sharp transitions that we
saw when

was applied to the reduced order nonlinear model.
